# 3D Workshop delivery

## Phase 1 — 150 published Guided Builds

Current verified total: **83 / 150 workshops**. **67** remain in Phase 1; **61** additional guides remain in Phase 2. Counts and in-progress descriptions in earlier checkpoint notes describe those earlier states.

Latest completed: **Arduino Automatic Tap System (`directory-27`)**. Next in catalog order: **Obstacle-avoiding car & 6-axis robotic arm (`directory-35`)**.

Use the user-approved obstacle-avoiding car as the visual and interaction benchmark. Add and verify a project-specific sixth 3D Workshop for each of the 150 published guides, continuing in catalog order.

Every workshop needs detailed relevant components, clear selectable part labels, connection explanations, animated assembly, a complete working demonstration, and the Atlas purple/navy interface theme. Verify navigation, actual geometry selection and highlighting, assembly animation, observable behaviour, and desktop/mobile layout locally.

The user approved the refined rover standard and authorized rollout to the other 149 projects.

GitHub delivery: the user requested ongoing pushes. Push source checkpoints after verified milestones to `Srikanthsri24/AI-AND-ROBOTICS-ATLAS` on `main`. Keep unfinished workshops unregistered and explicitly excluded from completed counts; do not force-push over remote changes.

In-progress checkpoint: Electromagnet Crane Working Model (`directory-20`) remains unregistered and excluded from the 75 completed workshops. Its controller now models separate coil power, button debounce, startup release-to-arm, wiring states and the eight-second cutoff. Fixed the downloadable sketch so repeated button bounce cannot postpone the cutoff. Six focused tests pass, including execution of the compiled downloadable sketch against mixed input timelines; targeted strict TypeScript checks pass. Physical workshop geometry and browser verification remain pending.

Crane physical checkpoint (still unregistered): added a project-specific braced timber gantry, clear sliding saddle, lifting cord, rear screw/bracket, hollow steel magnet cup and separate pole face, open-hole steel washer, pickup pad and shallow receiving tray. Added a detailed separate driver with copper/pad positions derived from Adafruit Eagle CAD, all nine PCB holes open, AO3406, pull-down, flyback, separate indicator lenses and open connector housings; source attribution and hardware license are included under `public/licenses`. Authored 23 explanatory part records, including an explicitly illustrative field overlay. Fixed-step motion requires face contact to collect the washer, carries the actual mesh with the hoist/trolley, releases under gravity and counts only landed tray deliveries. Tray walls block low travel/lowering, including the carried washer thickness. Twelve focused controller/motion tests pass, including compiled-sketch parity; targeted strict TypeScript passes. `scripts/inspect-crane-hardware.cjs` verifies actual GPU openings, saddle/crossbar clearance, washer pickup/travel/landing and actual-pointer selection plus material highlighting on all 22 physical groups at 1200 px and 390 px. This fixture proves the physical subsystem only: complete scene wiring, on-surface labels, staged assembly, guide UI and full workshop production-route verification remain pending. Completed total remains 75/150.

Crane controller-circuit checkpoint (still unregistered, total unchanged at 75/150): added the detailed USB-powered Uno and insulating feet, a real 170-hole breadboard, a four-foot tactile hold switch with an open retaining-cover hole and moving actuator, a keyed three-way driver plug, independent regulated 5 V supply, low-voltage cord and centre-positive 5.5 / 2.1 mm barrel adapter with separate recessed screw clamps. Eight individually identified leads reach the actual D2 / D9 / GND sockets, separate button strips and distinct positive / common-return banks. Verified actual rendered metal contact at all 16 ends (11 pin ends, three JST crimps, two screw clamps), all four button feet, four Uno supports, two driver supports and both adapter lid holes with `scripts/inspect-crane-contacts.cjs`. All eight seated jacket routes clear one another; minimum measured route clearance is about 0.998 mm. Corrected the driver input solder-tail direction to match its CAD pads. Added project-specific explanations for the controller, supply, button and leads. A new surface-label component follows the actual clicked triangle and does not intercept later clicks; stopped pointer propagation so rear geometry cannot overwrite the selected surface. `scripts/inspect-crane-hardware.cjs` passes actual pointer selection, actual material highlighting, explanatory-ID coverage and surface-anchor coincidence on all 55 exposed physical owners at 1200 px and 390 px. The DC centre pin remains inside its mated barrel and is explicitly excluded from exposed-surface clicking. `scripts/inspect-crane-label-motion.cjs` passes on both widths: the clicked washer triangle stays labelled through contact pickup, hoisting, crossbar transfer, gravity release and an off-pad timber landing. Landing heights now follow actual timber (10 mm), pad / tray floor (12 mm) and rim tops (16 mm), rather than leaving an off-pad load floating. Fifteen focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. Remaining before registration: flexible moving coil leads and their real connections, physical USB power connection, staged assembly and withdrawal motions, complete operating / transfer / timeout lesson UI, final visual polish and production-route verification.

Eight assembly stages, selectable soil/probe/resistor/LED geometry, lift-before-travel probe animation, dry/damp calibration, equal-reference rejection, midpoint LED boundary, automatic LED demo, depth-change checks and reset on desktop/mobile. Refined in this pass: labels now follow the actual moving blade / upper circuit and real part surfaces; all eight part groups pass actual mesh clicking on both widths. Added close-up / whole-bench controls, corrected AOUT / VCC / GND connector order and Uno contact positions, insulating board supports, a USB lead and drilled LED carrier. Assembly controls use actual mount settling. Dry / damp captures require the correct cup. Changing depth invalidates the displayed relative index and keeps the actual LED off until both references match again. The demo changes simulated sample moisture gradually, with observed physical LED ON / OFF transitions. The lens retains its red output colour while a separate violet ring shows selection. Three regression tests cover invalid / reversed / clamped calibration and the exact midpoint.
  - November 24 refinement: added a one-click five-scene guided calibration demonstration in the Atlas violet theme. - Unique detailed parts include drilled acrylic chassis, clamped TT gearmotors, hollow treaded tyres / bored five-spoke hubs, independent powered wheels, passive bored-fork swivel caster, correctly aligned insulating supports and delayed screws, four-AA holder / real output tabs / motor switch, USB bank / USB-A and USB-B plugs, closed animated power straps and a 170-point mini breadboard with thirty-four separate five-hole ties.
- Manufacturer board CAD informs actual Uno R3, Bluefruit and driver outlines, mounting holes, socket / pin positions, components and copper routes. The Uno is the AVR ATmega328P R3, not an R4 substitute. Geometry helpers retain official source URIs, hashes and CC BY-SA attribution. Breakouts and Uno use the same physical millimetre scale and 2.54 mm header pitch. Top copper routes are merged per PCB without dropping their paths.

Driver VCC and VM stay separate. The switched four-AA supply returns directly to driver ground, bypassing the small breadboard ties. Actual motor-lead endpoints swap in polarity fault tests, and the CTS ground plug physically lifts when disconnected.

Labels follow actual hit surfaces, including moving wheel surfaces; transparent annotation overlays do not intercept nearby wire clicks. Corrected inherited shell nav sizing, mobile header clipping and button colour specificity; final native route checks show no overflow or overlapping introduction / stage navigation.
- The frontend controller mirrors the published Arduino C++ receive-only SoftwareSerial sketch: one UART byte per loop, uppercase F / B / L / R / S, PWM 110, 850 ms drive timeout, unsupported byte stop and CR / LF ignored without refreshing the deadline. Diagnostics explicitly identify themselves as frontend inspection, not firmware acknowledgements. Actual radio MODE uses two Data flashes / three CMD flashes followed by the manufacturer’s pause; CONN follows the simulated link.
- All sixteen assembly stages, six physical wheel / Stop / communication-loss checks, power-off lift / stand removal / floor lowering and return-to-support handling pass on both widths. The complete car moves, pivots and coasts; the passive caster swivels / rolls only on the floor. Imperative wheel / caster transforms preserve detailed geometry during motion. ### LiDAR micro drone with proximity sensing — completed

`directory-5` now has its sixth **3D Workshop** route, retaining all five existing guide stages. It uses independently authored Crazyflie 2.1+ airframe / motors / factory wiring, a stock 250 mAh cell, upper Multi-ranger, lower Flow deck V2, both medium pin strips, a Crazyradio 2.0 USB dongle / articulated antenna, computer USB socket, four open-sided supports and a matte-card ranging bench. Source photos and published dimensions inform the shapes; internal placements, card supports and computer form remain authored visual approximations, not supplier CAD.

- Eight reversible assembly stages seat the lower deck, disconnected cell, upper deck, both straight pin strips, keyed battery connector and USB radio before presenting the target. Upper placement waits for the cell; pins wait for the aligned boards; power connection waits for seated pins. The verified medium stack is 15 + 2 + 2 mm at 2 mm pitch. Assembly / mode changes are interlocked with power, logger state and actual movement. A 71-position rendered sweep confirms deck / cell clearance, fixed board connector position, work-surface clearance and real support contact. Production mobile playback visits all eight stages.
- Deck geometry retains 24 upper-board openings, 20 lower-board bores, both hollow socket rows, spring contacts, five outward / upward VL53L1X packages, the downward PMW3901 / ToF windows, PCA9534 expander and support electronics. The flight board has 20 expansion bores plus two rear power pads, separate processors, sensing / supply parts, a hollow Micro-USB socket and four bored motor holders. Final processor refinement uses a 6 × 6 × 0.85 mm QFN48 with 0.4 mm contact pitch for nRF51822, distinct from the underside LQFP flight processor. Native production checks verify its 48 flat contacts, body and actual surface picking on both widths.
- The full 20 × 7 × 30 mm cell, hollow keyed connector halves and 19 mm pin strips fit the physical stack. Its two flexible cell leads remain 20 mm through 21 mating positions. All eight factory motor leads now retain the published 32 mm length; 4104 actual rendered path samples clear the flight PCB and motor cans. The motor shafts use 0.8 mm diameter / 3.5 mm length. Virtual-only 47 mm, two-blade propellers use independent twisted surfaces, correct opposing hands and bored hubs. They are absent from the real bench procedure.
- The radio has a hollow USB-A shell, four actual mating contacts, RF shield, RP-SMA coupling, duck antenna, bootloader button and support components. Its 63 × 18 × 8 mm board / connector envelope follows Bitcraze. Rendered contacts align at 4.4 mm height with 4 mm longitudinal engagement. USB powers the dongle separately from drone battery power; the drone link is wireless.
- The ruler is zeroed at the actual front optical face. The rendered card surface intersects that ray at 350 / 450 / 550 / 750 mm. Moving the card sideways physically takes it out of view and produces no valid return. The dotted line is explicitly an illustrative centre ray, not visible laser light or a full FoV / reflectance prediction.
- The host mirror matches the included Python logger’s 100 ms sampling, one-slot latest-packet queue, overwrite behaviour, pause / resume, 450 ms timeout / strict receipt-age cutoff, invalid-return Hold and 400 / 500 mm hysteresis. The real bench stays stationary with all motors stopped. Calibration accepts only fresh, ordered, actual logger rows after a target change: 750 ADVANCE → 350 HOLD → 450 HOLD → 550 ADVANCE. It approaches the actual card plane, reaches approximately 398 mm, holds and does not resume on invalid data. The Python program prints decisions only and sends no flight commands. The computer screen and scene HUD display the current proposal / logger state.

Inspection exposes actual underside surfaces while keeping the camera above the table. Covered processors / the cell label are uncovered by reversing the unpowered assembly; powered / running inspection requests explain the required stop / power-off order. Static geometry is memoized so factory cable curves are not rebuilt on every telemetry repaint.

### 62. DIY Vacuum Cleaner Using a Recycled Plastic Bottle — completed

Published at `#/project-builder/directory-6/workshop`. The sixth stage follows the existing air-only hand-bulb guide and its five photographed component families. It introduces no Arduino, motor or fictitious program.

- Independently authored clear, hollow PET bottle, ribbed white two-bore cap, mating neck / cap threads, integral rim seal, two hollow rubber grommets, long / short clear hoses, woven outlet pouch / retaining tie, removable partial cover and elastic black bulb with hollow barbed fittings. Separate workbench fixtures support the bottle, removed lid and rigid pump collars. The illustrative moving ball-seat valves explain one-way function; the vendor’s internal construction is unknown and is not claimed to be reproduced.
- Fourteen animated assembly stages fold the incoming grommet flanges before they enter the real 14 mm bores and let them reopen behind the 2.5 mm lid plate. The pickup end feeds coaxially through the port before bending inside. Its outer slack loop changes shape while keeping the full hose at 700 mm. The short outlet slides through its independent upper port; the loose pouch encloses its mouth and the tie tightens behind it. Actual rendered tie states conserve loop circumference plus tail length.
- Closing the bottle lifts it clear, aligns the neck, then advances it 9.6 mm over three turns while the hose-equipped lid stays still. The bottle, lid and both hoses return together before the pump is connected. This avoids winding the hoses around a turning lid. The removed-cap jig actually supports the 23.6 mm skirt bottom; bottle clearance, soft cradle contact and open cap / grommet bores are checked. The pump-side hose opens over the barb crests while retaining its 2 mm wall.
- Two isothermal gas cavities use an implicit mass-balance solve with active inlet / exhaust check valves. The bottle’s 1090.52 mL cavity and the bulb’s 94.94 → 34.45 mL compression volume derive from the same rendered profiles. Elastic recovery works against actual computed inside pressure, so covered mesh slows refill and a rim leak diverts intake. Resistances, spring response and dimensions are authored teaching parameters, not measured pump ratings.
- Six individually tracked 2.5 × 2.5 × 0.03 mm paper sheets move under the authored nozzle-air / drag / gravity model. A sheet is collected only after traversing the whole hose and crossing its actual discharge into the cavity. Eight-corner cavity checks and oriented-box contacts keep settled sheets inside the bottle without coincident geometry. In the reference clear-filter trial, all six targeted sheets enter the bottle; the covered-filter and leaking-cap reference cycles leave the target on the tray. The comparison is illustrative, not a prediction of real pickup performance.
- The purple / navy interface provides assembly navigation / replay, actual part descriptions and connection explanations, whole-bench and pump close-ups, conceptual cutaways, six distinct nozzle marks, one-cycle / six-mark playback, pause / resume / reset, gap adjustment and observations. Duplicate marks and incomplete records are rejected. Gap changes clear comparisons. CSV values come from six completed cycles and actual sheet states, including mean recovery and peak nozzle flow; they are not preset success counts. A visible one-fifth-turn / 0.64 mm authored rim gap accompanies the leak condition.
- All **28 physical selection groups** pass actual mesh clicking, physical material highlights and matching surface labels at 1440 and 390 widths. The labels use triangle barycentric coordinates, so they stay on the actual surface when vertices deform or workpieces move. Both hose endpoints are labelled. Covered parts are uncovered by reversing the idle assembly. Focus distance accounts for the narrow canvas; sampled selected-component vertices remain in view, and labels are bounded and non-overlapping without horizontal page overflow.
- The optional Follow paper camera tracks the real sheet through tray / hose / bottle, with its current location in the surface label and optional 0.25× playback. Whole bench restores orbiting. Phone Run / resume brings the complete canvas below the fixed header, and an in-view pause control avoids scrolling away from the animation. The built phone check observed the final sheet at the tracked camera target with the label “Paper sheet 1 · inside bottle”; the canvas occupied y≈215–705 in a 1000 px viewport. These are native geometry / behaviour checks, not screenshot-only assertions.
- **637 repository tests pass**, including **39 vacuum-specific pressure / volume, hose / fit, assembly, paper / contact, trial / record and surface-anchor tests**. TypeScript and production Vite build pass. Production desktop and phone flows verify six actual pickups, pause / resume, CSV download, visible leak gap and slower covered-filter recovery with no page errors. Production phone mesh checks also verify browser Back restores the overview. No backend or remote model / HDRI service is required.

### Automatic flood gate — controller / linkage foundation in progress

Started `directory-9` from the existing two-channel Arduino / servo lesson. Added an unregistered deterministic runtime with separate upstream / downstream physical gaps and uploaded references, sequential echo latching, a 6 ms unavailable timeout, and both explicit 60 ms sketch waits. Completed decision snapshots remain coherent while the next scan begins. Millimetre decisions match the published lesson's centimetre policy across **20,402 depth / prior-state combinations**: 80 mm opens, 70 mm holds, 50 mm recovers, and downstream capacity / sensor faults take priority.

Servo command and illustrated mechanical position are separate: travel is gradual at an explicitly authored 120°/s visual rate, and loss of either supply does not falsely animate guaranteed closure. Timing omits UART buffering / CPU overhead; no hydraulic flow or load / torque model is claimed. The 65–120° command range drives a custom circle-intersection linkage with a single fixed-length rod, not an independently rotated decorative flap. Its 551 checked poses retain both the rod length and the flap attachment radius throughout the travel.

### Smart-bin staged physical assembly — unregistered
Added eight project-specific assembly stages and motion for every owned component. Fixed hinge sleeves precede the lowering lid and axial hinge-pin insertion. The lid opens before the underside sensor assembly, then closes before the contact mechanism is fitted. Sensor bosses, plate, spacers, module and screws seat in order. Lower rubber lips compress for passage through the lid and relax only after seating; guide collars fit afterwards.

Resolved actual assembly-envelope issues rather than animating impossible fits: enlarged the four relaxed passages / guide bores to clear a 2.2 mm square precrimped housing, with updated spacing and flange clearance; split the bored roller wheel and separate axle, adding an actual fork with open ear bores; shortened the fork support arms so they do not fill those bores. The wheel lowers between the ears before the axle slides through. The final lowering striker gradually makes contact and depresses the roller/plunger. Also separated Uno screws from the insulating feet: the board seats on the bare feet before its larger screw heads and shield stack are fitted.

Ten circuit leads seat their two actual connector ends sequentially, followed by the two Ethernet cables. The lid opens for wiring, its fixed-length service loops follow it, and it closes for the final check. USB, load and powered collection indication are withheld during construction. Stable cable geometry is cached before/after wiring instead of rebuilding every hidden tube throughout unrelated assembly steps.

Current registry: **83 unique actual owners**. **39 focused smart-bin tests pass**, including all stage mappings, open/close order, grommet relaxation, wheel/axle/striker order, board/screw/shield order, insertion sequencing and cache equivalence. Prototype/data standalone TypeScript passes. Follow-up native checks after caching cover the wiring / lid transitions (`/tmp/atlas-bin-assembly-final.log`). ### Industrial IoT independent receiver and Ethernet foundation — unregistered
Added a fixed-1-ms session scheduler joining the source-verified controller to an independently powered read-only receiver. A service outage drops newly transmitted copies while local trip/LED evaluation continues. Restoring the service waits for the next new packet and never replays a packet lost during downtime. USB reset clears only the board and receiver deduplication sequence; world time, the last remote record and its receipt timestamp remain independent. The browser freshness boundary is exactly 2000 ms since the last accepted record; the supplied Python monitor separately uses socket receive timeout.

Added the authored two-port LAN switch, open receiver jack with eight contacts, separate link lamps, keyboard/touchpad and two Ethernet cables. Four plug ends seat separately; the receiver end uses a shorter withdrawal so its tail stays outside the breadboard during insertion. Cable routing clears the controller, switch case, knob plate, board and bench through staged poses. A shield unplug moves only that cable’s panel end. The physical cables stay unchanged during a receiver-service outage.

The monitor now decodes five clearly labelled copied fields: state, temperature index, vibration index, stop-loop condition and board uptime. ### Emergency Warning and Lighting controller foundation — unregistered (#70 in progress)
Started `directory-14` from `emergencyProgram.source`, not from another workshop's generic rules. The exported controller rounds the 10-bit A0 reading exactly, sets dark mode at index ≤ 30 and clears it at ≥ 42 while retaining either state in between. D2 Alarm acts immediately and captures the 500 ms pulse epoch only when first latched. D3 Reset qualifies its raw HIGH/LOW transition for 30 ms; an accepted reset while Alarm is held is consumed and cannot clear later without release and a new qualified press. Clearing an alarm in darkness leaves the three path lamps on. D8 and the 1250 Hz D9 command pulse together; Serial Monitor reports the exact source wording every 250 ms. Pausing freezes debounce/pulse timing; USB off clears the board and gates all outputs.

**Eight focused tests pass with no skips**, including **7,202 loop-by-loop comparisons executing the original sketch in C++** against the browser controller. Comparisons cover each of the three actual D5/D6/D7 outputs, red D8, tone/noTone, latch, raw/stable Reset, pulse start, serial cadence/text and uint32 rollover, with deterministic bounce/held-button cases and a later seeded input trace. Only host unsigned-long width is adapted; sketch control flow is unchanged. Exact ADC boundaries are 312→30, 313→31, 424→41 and 425→42. Targeted strict core TypeScript passes (`/tmp/atlas-emergency-core-tests.log`, `/tmp/atlas-emergency-core-tsc.log`).

Next: inspect the existing five-stage corridor reference/demo; author the dry cardboard corridor, elevated light divider, three independent white LED/resistor branches, entrance red branch, two separately wired tactile buttons, series-limited passive piezo, Uno/USB and the actual connected conductors. Then staged assembly, surface labels, optional user-enabled tone, operational lesson, full source/build and production desktop/phone checks. No new sixth tab is registered. ### Weather Imaging CubeSat — mission controller and open chassis, unregistered (#71 in progress)
Started `directory-15` from its existing Python mission. The typed controller captures only when Earth-facing and its single-frame buffer is free. Each captured frame freezes the original longitude, cloud setting, model capture time and rendered image string; later input changes cannot alter it. Downlink uses the guide's inclusive 35° E–85° E window, appends the same retained frame to an immutable archive and frees the buffer. Current pointing does not block transmission of an already stored frame. A display-only pass animation advances one authored degree per 250 model ms, pauses deterministically and stops at 120° E; its clock stops at the same endpoint under different frame subdivisions. This is explicitly a classroom longitude-window model, not orbital propagation.

**Four focused tests pass without skips**, including **600 seeded operations executing the original Python source** and comparing retained-buffer/archive metadata after every capture, setting change and downlink. Tests also cover occupied-buffer rejection, capture pointing, unchanged stored pixels, inclusive contact boundaries, settings bounds, pause and end-of-pass subdivision. Evidence: `/tmp/atlas-cubesat-core-tests.log`; targeted strict TypeScript: `/tmp/atlas-cubesat-core-tsc.log`.

Added ten explained chassis owner groups: four individually selectable L-profile corner rails with actual axial bores; open upper/lower frames with matching holes; separate upper/lower retaining screws; an open service cradle and a tabletop. The 98 mm rail span seats between the lower frame's top at 32 mm and upper frame's underside at 130 mm, avoiding an initial lower-frame overlap. The cradle contacts the lower frame at its edges while leaving the bus centre open. Dimensions are authored teaching geometry, not flight-qualified CAD.

Native Chrome verifies the actual four rail/frame bore axes and screw clearance, open central inspection aperture and rail/frame contact heights. **All ten actual owner groups pass native mesh clicking and material highlighting** (`/tmp/atlas-cubesat-chassis-clicks.log`). Visual: `/tmp/atlas-cubesat-chassis.png`; targeted strict chassis TypeScript passes (`/tmp/atlas-cubesat-chassis-tsc.log`). The spacecraft is still incomplete: camera/payload, electronics and memory, solar surfaces/antenna, ground station, mechanical assembly, real rendered-frame capture, mission/downlink animations and the student interface remain pending, followed by full desktop/mobile production checks. #### Ten-chapter physical assembly engine checkpoint
Added the actual 0–9 assembly timeline and ten student chapter instructions. The base rests on four temporary supports while posts and underside screws are fitted. It lifts 2 mm clear of those blocks before they slide out, then lowers onto its own feet. The prewired servo module, its three fixed leads and connector rest move together onto the posts. Ten power/signal/reference plugs seat individually. USB then connects and the **bare shaft** follows the original sketch’s setup request to 90° within a 1.2-second authored sample of its 1.5-second startup delay. The servo output switches off and the Uno end disconnects before horn or sensor fitting.

The horn lowers over the centred shaft before its retaining screw enters. The bracket lowers separately; its nuts slide underneath the horn at a fixed accessible height before the screws descend. Sensor nuts approach from behind the straps, followed by front screws. The eight sonar plugs seat individually, the target stand lowers to the bench and USB reconnects with servo output still off. Split internal screw/nut group names preserve all original physical owner IDs and final geometry. There are **91 working hardware owners**, plus one selectable temporary-support owner during assembly (**92** in the assembly catalogue). The supports leave the visible bench after use.

#### Railway Gate assembly foundation checkpoint

#### Railway Gate opposite-side fastener assembly checkpoint
Split servo-ear and card-arm fasteners into independent front-bolt and rear-nut transforms, while preserving their existing selectable owner groups. Stand feet seat first; front mounting bolts enter their real bores before the rear nuts seat. The arm bolts now have actual recessed driver slots and two-turn fitting cues. MQ-2 timing checkpoint (unregistered; 76/150 unchanged): added immediate boot sampling followed by nominal 100 ms loop boundaries, queued serial baseline commands, USB power-cycle RAM reset, and explicit uncertainty after floating A0 / missing common ground. MQ-2 circuit checkpoint (unregistered; 76/150 unchanged): added explicit voltage-divider and dial calculations with ideal AVR 1024-bin ADC conversion. The equal 10 kΩ divider halves module AOUT; dial and divider cannot drive A0 simultaneously. Missing ground, power, signal path, invalid resistances and over-reference A0 remain unknown with actionable explanations. No gas / ppm / heater response curve is invented. Ten focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. Manufacturer research confirms Waveshare module dimensions 40 × 21 mm, 2 mm fixing holes, and DOUT / AOUT / GND / VCC interfaces: https://www.waveshare.com/mq-2-gas-sensor.htm . ## Rain-protection clothesline controller checkpoint — in progress

Started `directory-22` with a controller matching its downloadable Arduino sketch: startup target 35°, immediate covered command on a wet reading, 130° extension only at four continuous dry seconds, repeated wet samples resetting that deadline, change-only Servo writes and 250 ms serial reporting. Unsigned subtraction matches the AVR 32-bit `millis()` rollover. `commandAngle` is explicitly a target command, not instantaneous servo / carriage position.

Five focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. The native compiled-sketch comparison covers 409 events, including dry deadlines, repeated wet readings, serial output, initial write-before-attach order and rollover. The harness preserves the original source text and adapts native `unsigned long` width to AVR's 32-bit ABI after standard headers. Sensor / servo geometry, carriage / linkage mechanics, power / wiring, assembly, labels, working demonstration and application verification remain pending. The workshop is unregistered and excluded from completion: **77/150**, with **73 Phase 1 workshops** and **61 Phase 2 guides** pending.

Rain clothesline mechanical checkpoint (unregistered; 77/150 unchanged): added a project-specific card base, parallel raised rails, open-channel carriage guides, canopy, moving clothesline / pegs / paper shirts, authored micro-servo envelope, horn, fixed-length 65 mm pushrod and pinned joints. The horn-to-slider kinematics retain a 20 mm crank radius and monotonically extend the carriage between the sketch's 35° / 130° commands. The paper load lies under the roof when covered and clears it when extended. Mechanical response is a deliberately slowed authored visual rate, separately represented from the controller's target command; it is not a measured servo performance claim.

Eight focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. The native geometry fixture verifies 20 actual rendered poses: horn-tip alignment, both rod endpoints, carriage movement and genuinely open guide channels with solid upper walls. Reviewed covered / extended screenshots. This remains a mechanical prototype: detailed servo hardware, sensor / tray / water behavior, power and circuit wiring, explanatory labels, assembly and complete operating interface / route checks are pending. No completed-count increase.

Rain water-sensor checkpoint (unregistered; 77/150 unchanged): researched Seeed's Grove Water Sensor 101020018. The manufacturer product page gives a 40 × 20 mm body; its wiki describes the 1 MΩ pull-up, LOW when conductive water bridges sensing / ground traces, and GND / VCC / unused / SIG cable connections. The wiki's legacy Eagle archive is binary, so component / ear placement is explicitly photo-estimated rather than claimed as extracted fabrication CAD. Sources: https://www.seeedstudio.com/Grove-Water-Sensor.html and https://wiki.seeedstudio.com/Grove-Water_Sensor/ .

Added a board-specific 3D prototype with three open mounting ears, edge notches, 11 separate plated electrode strips, via / mounting rings, pull-up resistor and four-pin socket. Added an ideal geometric bridge model: a drop touching plain board or only one network does not trigger a bridge; overlapping drops can join the two networks. Tangency without contact area does not count. This is not a calibrated water-conductivity / level model and is not yet wired to the controller. Ten focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. Native geometry checks pass three open holes, separated metal strips, trace envelopes and four physical pins; reviewed the rendered board. Visible water / tray behavior, full electronics and connector mating, labels, assembly and integrated workshop verification remain pending.

Rain integrated water / motion checkpoint (unregistered; 77/150 unchanged): added a fixed 1 ms educational session model coupling ideal conductive-water bridge detection, active-low input, original controller deadlines and gradual servo / linkage motion. Controller target and actual authored mechanical angle remain separate. Separate servo power / ground / signal gate motion; missing sensor wiring stays unknown until a sketch reset. USB power loss clears controller state without teleporting the physical carriage. Corrected floating-point endpoint snapping so a completed visual stroke reaches its exact requested angle.

Added a separate shallow sensor tray, three supports and visible water caps / thin contact films. The native integrated fixture passes the plain-board negative case, visible bridging drop, covered command before physical movement, actual carriage retraction, blotting removal and four-second dry recovery before extension. Reviewed the wet-state render. All 13 focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass, including frame partition / fractional carry, independent servo power and unknown-input reset. Water geometry / conductivity remain illustrative, not a calibrated fluid simulation. Detailed servo / controller electronics, actual wires / power mating, drop application and blotting animations, explanatory callouts, assembly and the full student-facing interface / route remain pending. Rain clothes-protection workshop completed (`directory-22`; **78/150**): registered the project-specific lazy-loaded sixth tab after its development checks. The workshop includes the authored Uno, Grove water sensor with separate electrode networks, individually connected circuit wires / contact housings, separate servo supply with inspectable DC plug, detailed servo mounting hardware, fixed-length horn / pushrod linkage, guided carriage, paper clothes / pegs / canopy, USB plugs and computer serial display. Sixteen narrated chapters include physical fitting and source-first wiring; all power is off during assembly. Full playback, pause / resume, automatic USB connection, independent servo power, actual versus commanded position, wet bridging, plain-board negative control, four-second dry return, RESET, dynamic drop inspection and exact Arduino source download are verified.

All 76 static owners pass actual-surface focus / highlighting / labels and real pointer reselection after camera-assisted focus at desktop and phone widths; both water-drop controls have separate direct-click checks. The final integrated production build passes at 1440 and 390 pixels: overview-to-sixth-tab navigation, six stages, power / DC interlocks, wet and dry motion, dynamic label / removal, exact sketch bytes, no document overflow, fifth-stage navigation and browser-back remount to a fresh assembly. Reviewed the built desktop workshop rendering. Full repository TypeScript, Vite production build and all **1,102 repository tests** pass. The existing shared-chunk size advisory remains. Geometry / movement use explicitly authored classroom-model dimensions and rates; sampled native clearance checks are not manufacturer-CAD or continuous-volume certification. Earlier rain in-progress notes describe superseded checkpoints.

The workshop is now available locally at `http://127.0.0.1:5190/#/project-builder/directory-22/workshop`. No AWS deployment was performed. Phase 1 has **72 workshops remaining**; Phase 2 still has **61 additional guides**. Next catalog guide: RFID-Based Door Lock System (`directory-23`).

RFID door-lock controller checkpoint (`directory-23`; unregistered; 78/150 unchanged): implemented the original sketch loop as a project-specific controller with one volatile enrolled UID, exact length / byte matching, enrollment, rejection, three-second release / relock, two failed reads before a fresh presentation, held-tag lockout, unavailable-reader startup and uint32 millis rollover. Entry and reader-return timestamps are separate because the PN532 read can block; the release timestamp is captured after a successful read. LED writes occur before enrollment / scanning, retaining the original one-loop indication latency. Native compilation of the unchanged downloadable Arduino source matches learned UID, misses, enrollment / denied / unlocked flags, timer, servo command / write count, LED pins and emitted serial messages across 360 events. Five tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. Clarified the existing guide: D3 stays reserved for the reader-library reset parameter alongside D2 / A4 / A5. Primary constructor / reset reference: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/adafruit/Adafruit-PN532/master/Adafruit_PN532.cpp . No shield reset-wire connection is invented. Detailed PN532 shield geometry, model door / servo latch, components / wiring, assembly, tag placement, explanations, workshop UI and browser verification remain pending. No completed-count increase.

RFID door-mechanism checkpoint (`directory-23`; unregistered; 78/150 unchanged): added an authored tabletop base / frame, hinged card door with separate leaves / pins, knob, folded projecting strike, fixed positional servo, shaft, rotating latch tab and retaining screw. All seven RFID tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. Dimensions remain authored classroom geometry, not manufacturer CAD. Reader shield / tags, detailed hardware, electrical wiring, assembly / labels, full state integration and the workshop UI / route remain pending. No completed-count increase.

PN532 populated-surface checkpoint (`directory-23`; unregistered; 78/150 unchanged): added 67 individually selectable CAD-positioned surface items with 135 pads, including passives, crystal, regulator, level-shift transistors, board LEDs, test pads, fiducials and two selector footprints. RFID integrated-session checkpoint (`directory-23`; unregistered; 78/150 unchanged): split the verified original loop into start-of-loop GPIO / timer work and reader-return work, preserving the downloadable sketch parity across 360 events. Added deterministic one-ms integration with explicitly authored 180 ms reader cycles and 900 ms tag travel, queued serial enrollment commands, separate servo power / ground / signal gates and gradual physical latch movement. A tag handoff withdraws the previous tag before advancing the next. Holding an enrolled tag through the relock cannot retrigger; USB cycling forgets enrollment, RESET preserves the physical angle until commanded travel, and failed reader startup requires RESET to retry. The reader power LED follows USB power in the integrated prototype. Native browser verification follows animated tag state through enrollment A, rejection B, fresh A release, actual rendered intermediate latch angle, held-tag relock and USB power loss. Fifteen focused tests and targeted strict TypeScript pass. Read timing / presentation positions are illustrative, not measured reader performance. Workbench supports, independent supply / LED circuit / visible wires, full assembly / label / inspection controls and production route are still pending. No completed-count increase.

Smart Digital School Bell started (`directory-24`; unregistered; 79/150 unchanged): authored schedule/controller loop and separate RTC/Arduino power-time session. Three weekday slots ring once per changed minute; Saturday/Sunday and adjacent minutes stay quiet. The 25 ms loop yields 200 ms first tone, 100 ms silence and 275 ms second tone, rather than pretending nominal note durations bypass loop sampling. Test commands cancel an active sequence; boot inside a scheduled minute stays silent. RTC battery advances calendar while USB-powered controller/display are off; loss of both supplies freezes time/marks it untrusted until successful boot restores represented compile time. Valid RTC survives RESET; startup I²C failure requires another reset after restoration. Output presence gates do not change schedule logic. Ten focused tests and targeted strict TS pass. Fixed compile epoch is an authored example, not live wall-clock time. Compiled downloadable-sketch parity, actual RTC/display/piezo geometry, wiring, assembly, labels, audio/visual demonstration and all UI/production verification pending. Unregistered; no completion increase.

Automatic Liquid Soap Dispenser (`directory-26`) — controller/session foundation in progress; unregistered, **81/150 unchanged**. Added the exact four-phase WAIT_CLEAR/READY/PRESSING/REFILLING controller from the existing downloadable sketch: three clear readings at >=18 cm arm it; three readings in inclusive 4–12 cm request one 35°→65° press; 460 ms press and 350 ms refill require a new clear presentation before repeating. Missing echo prints -1 and cannot trigger/rearm; an already READY state is retained as in the sketch. Integer echo rounding, unsigned millis rollover, independent phase timing and the after-acquisition changed timestamp are preserved.

Added a timestamped servo session with 1 ms idle-loop scheduling and blocking sonar acquisition: 12 µs trigger preparation plus an ideal echo pulse, or a 7000 µs timeout. Phase changes occur before acquisition; range/qualifying updates occur at completion, while illustrated PWM travel can continue. The ideal read model claims no extra echo-start/UART latency. Source command/report counts stay distinct from `completedServoPresses`, which records the illustrated shaft actually reaching 65° after its return to 35°. It does not claim pump displacement, priming, liquid output or measured torque. Independent servo power/reference/signal faults hold the illustrated shaft, even when the sketch reports a stroke. USB resets logic without teleporting the shaft or erasing its physical movement history; fractional frame remainders preserve timing.

PASS twelve focused tests and strict/no-unused targeted TS. One harness compiles the exact downloaded Arduino C++ sketch and compares 802 physically ordered mixed loops, including echo rounding, reports, phase pins/command and rollover. Another runs over 2,000 automatically scheduled native loops over two presentations and compares the session state/report stream at the same acquisition-completion timestamps. Power, wiring, missing echo, one-cycle-per-presentation, USB reset and fractional-frame cases pass. Bottle/pump, bracket, sonar, wiring, actual-part inspection, assembly animation, working 3D dispense/return demo, UI and full production verification are still required before registration. Current next project remains directory-26; 69 Phase 1 workshops and 61 Phase 2 guides/workshops remain.

Soap dispenser bottle/pump checkpoint (`directory-26`, unregistered; **81/150 unchanged**): built a dedicated hollow reservoir matching the existing blue-soap component reference, with smooth shoulders, neck/closure helices and fluted collar. The pump has a fixed chamber, open dip tube, separate inlet/outlet balls and seats, sliding hollow stem/piston seal, compressing spring with flat end turns, hollow press head and an open curved nozzle. The virtual cutaway exposes the internal passages and valve phases. Fifteen physical owners each have function and connection explanations. Static profiles/settings are cached so moving the head does not reconstruct the reservoir and all fixed annuli.

The 0–6 mm head stroke moves the stem, piston, upper valve and nozzle together; spring end turns meet the lower seat and piston underside in every checked pose. Defined the next mechanical follower as a 12 mm left-pointing horn with an offset roller: the sketch's actual 35°/65° commands map to 0/6 mm, the roller stays on the bevelled head's contact plane, and its lateral movement remains within the head. This is geometry/illustrated travel, not a servo-torque, pressure, priming or liquid-volume calculation. The servo/follower hardware itself is not yet built. The reservoir illustration can show a level below the dip-tube intake rather than equating any nonzero fill with available liquid.

PASS sixteen focused soap tests (twelve controller/session/compiled-sketch cases plus four pump geometry/metadata cases) and targeted strict/no-unused TS. Native Chrome PASS seven full/partial strokes with actual rendered spring-bearing faces, coupled nozzle/stem/contact planes, open nozzle-mouth/head-bore rays and matching solid-rim/control rays. All fifteen actual surface picks, clicked-material highlights and labels PASS at 1200/390 with the cutaway open.
